### 2026-11-25 Alpha/Beta guest clock and timer comparison

- Recovered read-only Lima access with the application-bundled Lima 2.3.0 binary and the explicit lab `LIMA_HOME`; `limactl` is not on the current shell PATH. The active `/data/log/CubeVmm/vmm.log` had rotated and was empty, so the Beta restore record was in `/data/log/CubeVmm/vmm.log.1`. It specifies `highres=off clocksource=kvm-clock` and two vCPUs. The Cube VMM source comment beside `KVM_ARM_VCPU_PMU_V3` says that when the host does not support PMUv3, initialization retries without PMU and the guest PMU device is not advertised. The PMU warning is the documented fallback path, not evidence that PMU caused Chromium's stall.
- Read-only PTY inspections of current Alpha `74ef6039d5554c15afdb2555b055e5d3` (generation 20) and Beta `315bbf88bd0e497a92099d23845e7a55` (generation 19) found both running with `desktop_state=failed` and zero active runs. Both report guest kernel `6.6.119-cube.bm.guest.001`, 2 vCPUs, ARM64 CPU implementer `0x61` / part `0x000`, Chrome `158.0.8127.88`, and the same kernel command line. Each reports `arch_sys_counter` as the only available and active clocksource; `kvm-clock` was not selected. `/proc/timer_list` reports `hres_active: 0` and 4,000,000 ns resolution on both vCPUs. The Lima host also selects `arch_sys_counter`, with `hres_active: 1` and 1 ns resolution.
- Upstream Linux documents `highres=` as enabling/disabling high-resolution timer mode (default on), and `clocksource=` as selecting an override clocksource. The guest inspection confirms the explicit `highres=off` is active while the requested `kvm-clock` is unavailable. This is a real timing difference that could contribute to latency, but it has not been tested as Chromium's cause and is shared by the current Alpha and Beta guests, so it does not by itself explain their current desktop failure. The earlier Alpha DSH task on 2026-11-22 used snapshot `snap-4029ae11937541db8609a8b6`; the current Alpha VM was restored on 2026-11-25, so its current VM state is not a direct reproduction of that historical successful task.
- Chrome-version inspection was captured in `~/.local/share/csa-cube-node/build-logs/chrome-clock-diff-alpha-20261125T1230Z.log` and `chrome-clock-diff-beta-20261125T1230Z.log`. A first attempt also invoked the `/usr/bin/chromium` wrapper with `--version`; its shell compatibility error held the PTY until its 30-second bound. The diagnostic now queries only `/usr/bin/google-chrome --version` behind a 5-second timeout. No Chrome render, template update, VM create/restart, package install, or tenant profile change was made during this clock comparison.
- CubeShim v0.8.0 supports `cube.vm.kernel.cmdline.append` as a JSON array in sandbox annotations ([source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/TencentCloud/CubeSandbox/v0.8.0/CubeShim/shim/src/sandbox/config.rs)). The public CubeAPI `NewSandbox` model and `CreateTemplateRequest` do not expose arbitrary annotations; unknown sandbox fields are ignored, and the SDK's `extra` option cannot make CubeAPI forward an unsupported field ([sandbox model](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/TencentCloud/CubeSandbox/v0.8.0/CubeAPI/src/models/mod.rs), [template service](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/TencentCloud/CubeSandbox/v0.8.0/CubeAPI/src/services/templates.rs)). The CubeAPI-to-CubeMaster internal `CreateSandboxRequest` does have an annotations map, but the public service constructs that map itself. CubeMaster's internal `POST /cube/sandbox` accepts the map and merges it with template annotations; the merge copies template values over same-key request values ([internal request model](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/TencentCloud/CubeSandbox/v0.8.0/CubeAPI/src/cubemaster/mod.rs), [CubeMaster merge](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/TencentCloud/CubeSandbox/v0.8.0/CubeMaster/pkg/service/httpservice/cube/cubeboxutil.go)). This is a possible diagnostic route only if access path, authentication, and exposure are verified first. None has been verified, and no such request has been sent.
- The one authorized 2-vCPU/2048-MiB disposable sandbox after the XFS expansion was created and cleaned up. Its guest file stage failed at elapsed 4.8 seconds with `fetch failed`; it did not test the timer candidate. That one-off launch is already consumed. No additional diagnostic VM has been created, and no tenant VM was changed during the clock comparison.
- Next use read-only evidence to verify CubeMaster's internal route exposure and authentication, then decide whether the timer candidate is worth an isolated test. Do not send `annotations` through public `Sandbox.create.extra` and assume they reached CubeShim; do not rebuild or restart either tenant VM based only on the timer candidate. The one-off disposable VM authorization described above has already been used.

### 2026-11-25 CubeMaster route exposure and local reachability

- CubeSandbox v0.8.0 mounts `/cube/*` routes through the request middleware. Its `checkAuth` function skips signature validation when `AuthConf.Enable` is false ([server route setup](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/TencentCloud/CubeSandbox/v0.8.0/CubeMaster/pkg/server/server.go), [auth middleware](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/TencentCloud/CubeSandbox/v0.8.0/CubeMaster/pkg/service/httpservice/middleware/middleware.go)).
- Read-only inspection of the active node found `common.http_bind: 0.0.0.0`, `common.http_port: 8089`, and `auth.enable: false`; `ss` confirmed CubeMaster listening on `*:8089`. An unauthenticated GET `/cube/capabilities` returned application `ret_code=200` and two snapshot modes, confirming this route is currently unauthenticated.
- The Mac's direct request to `192.168.5.15:8089/cube/capabilities` timed out. ### 2026-11-25 official CubeSandbox Chromium performance report

- CubeSandbox issue [#1505](https://github.com/TencentCloud/CubeSandbox/issues/1505) reports Chromium running smoothly in Docker but poorly in a Cube desktop. It is not a reproduction of this incident: #1505 measures animated-page/VNC frame performance under x86 PVM, while the current ARM64 Lima/KVM guest fails to render a local data URL in headless Chrome before CDP responds. Do not use #1505 as proof of this failure's cause. A native ARM64 KVM baseline would separate Mac→Lima nesting from Cube guest behavior, but this work has not provisioned or used another host.

### 2026-11-25 CubeShim ARM64 boot-parameter source audit

- Read-only inspection of the deployed v0.8.0 source at `/data/cubelet/csa-cube-shim-build-20261124-41748bb/source/CubeShim` found the ordinary sandbox boot path in `shim/src/sandbox/sb.rs` adds `highres=off` and `clocksource=kvm-clock` unconditionally. `cube.vm.kernel.cmdline.append` values are checked for duplicate parameter keys afterward, so an annotation cannot override either value by appending a second setting.
- `shim/src/snapshot/mod.rs::do_snapshot()` is the source-snapshot builder; its tap branch adds those same two parameters, while its other branch adds `clocksource=tsc` and `tsc=reliable`. Sandbox launch itself is handled by `shim/src/sandbox/sb.rs::start_vm()`: it first tries `restore_vm()` for an available snapshot and only calls `boot_vm()` after restore fails. The Beta CubeShim log `cube-shim-req.log.1` contains a `snapshot dir` record for sandbox `315bbf88bd0e497a92099d23845e7a55`, confirming it entered the snapshot-restore path. A change limited to `sb.rs::prepare_resource()` would not change the already-created snapshot Beta restores; an effective timer experiment must also change snapshot construction and rebuild the template.
- The candidate patch in `patches/cubesandbox-v0.8.0-arm64-native-clock.patch` now covers both ordinary sandbox boot and source-snapshot construction. `patch --dry-run` against the deployed v0.8.0 source passed. It was applied only to the staged source tree, then `cargo test --offline -p containerd-shim-cube-rs` passed 84/84 tests, including the new architecture-specific snapshot clock test. The patch was then reversed; SHA-256 of both source files matches the pre-test values. No binary was installed and no VM was started. The 4 ms guest timer resolution remains a possible contributor; no experiment has shown that it causes Chrome's missing render/CDP response.
- The internal CubeMaster annotation route is unauthenticated in the current node configuration, and direct Mac access to it timed out. Do not use that route to inject boot parameters. The previously authorized disposable launch has already been consumed; no further VM was created for this audit.
- Build log: `/data/cubelet/csa-cube-shim-build-20261124-41748bb/build-logs/arm64-native-clock-cargo-test-20261125.log`. Cargo was not on the node's default PATH, but a staged Rust 1.77.2 toolchain, 187 MiB registry cache, and existing target cache were available and reused. The test grew the Cube XFS use from 60 to 63 GiB; it was not followed by cleanup.
- Current capacity check after the test: the dedicated 90 GiB lab volume has 8.4 GiB free, below this project's 12 GiB node-start preflight threshold; Lima root has 3.5 GiB free and the Cube XFS disk has 18 GiB free. A template rebuild and runtime experiment were not started. Preserve the build cache and existing backups; resolve the lab-volume headroom before requesting another VM.

### 2026-11-25 host-side resize preflight and activity-feed regression

- Read-only `hdiutil info` confirms `/Users/shangguan/Library/Application Support/Coke Super Agent/lab/cube-node-lab.sparsebundle` remains attached as `/dev/disk5`, with APFS physical store `/dev/disk5s2` and volume `/dev/disk6s1` mounted at `/Users/shangguan/.local/share/csa-cube-node`. `diskutil apfs list` reports a 96.6 GB virtual device (90 GiB), 89.6 GB in use, and 7.0 GB unallocated inside the APFS container.
- Read-only `hdiutil resize -limits -imageonly -size 110g <sparsebundle>` returned `hdiutil: resize: failed. Resource temporarily unavailable (35)` while the sparsebundle remained attached. No image or filesystem resize was attempted. `diskutil apfs resizeContainer disk6 limits` was also read-only; it reports the current 96.6 GB physical store and did not change it. Do not treat its reported partition-map ceiling as available capacity in the 90-GiB backing image. Apple’s `hdiutil(1)` documents that `-imageonly` changes the image size only and that it does not resize APFS filesystems; `diskutil apfs resizeContainer` is a separate live operation constrained by the physical store. Sources: [hdiutil(1)](https://manp.gs/mac/1/hdiutil), [diskutil(8)](https://keith.github.io/xcode-man-pages/diskutil.8.html).
- This did not find a reliable way to enlarge the sparsebundle while it is attached. Keep the resize blocked until the Docker Desktop mount holder can be released within an explicitly authorized maintenance window; do not force-detach or resize the APFS container beyond the backing image.

Docker, MySQL, K3D, Registry, and Cube node remained running. This verifies API behavior, not the microVM desktop or DSH task chain.

No Chrome fatal was recorded. `Browser.getVersion` on the pipe timed out at 332,973 ms, then the bridge shut down; the product bootstrap failed after about 476 seconds. This narrows the failure to a Chrome startup/CDP response stall, but the stderr does not explain why. Full log: `~/.local/share/csa-cube-node/build-logs/chrome-bridge-template-2026-11-26T03-28-33-472Z-9d7e56ea-24e6-4078-a69c-c3d2db2ad0cf.jsonl`.
- Disposable transport control `fd645182-4a24-4df3-bec9-826e223379a1` hid the bridge file only inside clone `4f8b4c6313144110abeef52423307190` and launched Chrome with ordinary `--remote-debugging-port=9222`. Chrome printed `DevTools listening`, but the endpoint never became reachable; by the 180-second readiness timeout there was no 9222 socket, while the Chrome main process was still alive. Memory was about 235 MB of 2 GiB and OOM remained zero. This rules out the custom pipe bridge as the sole cause. Log: `~/.local/share/csa-cube-node/build-logs/chrome-bridge-template-2026-11-26T03-38-35-354Z-fd645182-4a24-4df3-bec9-826e223379a1.jsonl`.
- The local Lima config uses `vmType: vz`, `nestedVirtualization: true`, and 8 GiB for the Linux host VM; `/dev/kvm` is present and the node KVM-only preflight passes. The Cube template itself is 2 vCPU/2 GiB. This confirms a nested ARM64 virtualization stack, not native bare-metal KVM. Upstream CubeSandbox documents that ARM64 bare-metal deployment needs native KVM and its discussion of GUI Chromium reports VNC lag under nested PVM; a follow-up attributes that report's animated-page case to software shadow-page-table handling and over 60,000 page faults per second ([ARM64 quick start](https://github.com/TencentCloud/CubeSandbox/blob/master/docs/guide/quickstart.md), [GUI performance issue #1505](https://github.com/TencentCloud/CubeSandbox/issues/1505)). That is relevant evidence but does not prove the same mechanism in this VZ/nested-KVM guest. The official [Browser Sandbox example](https://github.com/TencentCloud/CubeSandbox/blob/master/examples/browser-sandbox/README.md) uses a prestarted headless browser on the standard CDP port. Cube's [native Apple Virtualization backend request #975](https://github.com/TencentCloud/CubeSandbox/issues/975) has since been closed as not planned. These upstream reports make nested virtualization a plausible factor, but do not explain this specific startup stall; our standard-port clone also failed.
- The READY template, application template setting, and Alpha/Beta tenant sandboxes remain unchanged. Do not label this template desktop-ready or promote it while HTTP/WebSocket CDP control fails.

### 2026-11-26 runtime computer-use policy alignment

- The DSH runtime already launches with `DSH_PERMISSION_MODE=danger-full-access`, and its task rules allow routine browser, desktop, terminal and package-install actions without per-step approval. The shared Computer plugin provides owner handoff only for a real site sign-in or human verification challenge, then refuses subsequent computer actions while the handoff marker exists.
- Audit found the Pi runtime passed the user's task directly to `session.prompt()` without DSH's corresponding computer-use rules. Updated `scripts/cube/pi-runtime/run-task.mjs` to append the same policy, treat page/terminal/file content as untrusted, and include user prompt and policy character counts in the trace. This does not change tenant isolation or the sign-in/verification handoff behavior.
- `node --check scripts/cube/pi-runtime/run-task.mjs` and `git diff --check` passed. A new Pi runtime key will be produced on the next build/install because the runtime key includes `run-task.mjs`; this specific changed prompt has not yet been exercised inside a microVM.

### 2026-11-26 Cube upstream nested-KVM graphics evidence

- Re-read CubeSandbox issue [#1505](https://github.com/TencentCloud/CubeSandbox/issues/1505). Its reporter saw Chromium GUI lag inside Cube despite adequate CPU/memory, then measured more than 60,000 page faults per second on an animated SVG workload and attributed the case to software shadow-page-table handling in PVM; the discussion distinguishes Docker-on-L1 hardware-assisted paging from PVM's L2 overhead. This is relevant upstream evidence for the current Mac → Lima → Cube microVM graphics risk, but that report used PVM and its mechanism is not yet proven to be the cause in our VZ/nested-KVM guest.
- Cube issue [#975](https://github.com/TencentCloud/CubeSandbox/issues/975), proposing a native Apple Virtualization backend, is closed as not planned. The thread includes a separate M5 Pro/Lima follow-up that reports nested-KVM timing/readiness limits and proposed shim/readiness fixes; it does not establish usable Chromium/noVNC performance. Keep the local Chrome root cause open and do not promote the current template.
- Read the follow-up [PR #2345](https://github.com/TencentCloud/CubeSandbox/pull/2345), which remains Open: it proposes deriving CubeShim's `VsockServerReady` wait from the caller deadline capped at 120 s and increasing the template pre-snapshot readiness fallback from 100 ms to 1 s. The author reports 10.4 s and 38 s cold starts on M5 Pro, successful v0.8.0 template creation, and sequential sandbox creates in roughly 1.05–1.63 s. This is useful upstream evidence that Lima/nested-KVM cold-start and snapshot probes can fail on fixed deadlines. A bot review points out that the proposed 120 s shim cap equals Cubelet's 120 s create-stuck threshold and may let its dead-container scanner misclassify an in-progress create. That review was visible on the open PR; do not blindly replace the locally deployed 60 s shim patch with the unmerged 120 s proposal. None of these tests covers Chromium, XFCE, VNC or Browser/Computer Use. [PR conversation and review](https://github.com/TencentCloud/CubeSandbox/pull/2345)

### 2026-11-26 Chrome pipe write observed, no response

- Run `e8e24255-d55d-4694-a175-d9e25a281c69` did not reach Chrome: the diagnostic copied the raw `.mjs` bridge into `/tmp`, where Node could not resolve its bare `ws` dependency (`ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`). This was an instrumentation-harness failure, not evidence against the image. The harness now bundles the bridge with the image-pinned `ws@8.22.2` tarball from the local npm cache and runs syntax/import smoke checks before creating a clone.
- Run `8033756d-9b02-423e-aa56-5f3881698c0f` used a disposable sandbox `85beb0cb620846ffbe04d5076aea0902`; the clone was deleted successfully. system D-Bus worked. The bridge spawned Chrome PID 744 and wrote the 39-byte `Browser.getVersion` frame to its child-input socket: parent FD 26 mapped to `socket:[3519]`, `write()` returned `accepted=true`, and its callback completed. During the 30-second startup deadline the bridge received no pipe output chunk and no matching CDP response, then timed out after 36.1 seconds and cleaned up the desktop session. This narrows the failure to Chrome not producing a response after a successful parent-side write; it does not yet show whether Chrome inherited the intended FD 3/4 endpoints.
- Chromium defines the pipe protocol as Chrome reading FD 3 and writing FD 4, and Puppeteer uses child stdio `[3]` as its write stream and `[4]` as its read stream. Our bridge follows that same parent-side mapping; the next short run captures the Chrome child's FD 3/4 targets and status to verify inheritance before attributing the stall to Chromium or nested virtualization. [Chromium FD contract](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/main/content/public/browser/devtools_agent_host.h) · [Puppeteer pipe mapping](https://github.com/puppeteer/puppeteer/blob/main/packages/puppeteer-core/src/node/BrowserLauncher.ts)
- Host trace: `~/.local/share/csa-cube-node/build-logs/template-chrome-threads-2026-11-26T06-32-22-278Z-8033756d-9b02-423e-aa56-5f3881698c0f.jsonl`. The diagnostic records are for disposable clones only; the candidate image, template configuration, and tenant VMs were not modified.
- Run `0f7d9b2d-63df-452b-afdc-ac3f395776bc` extended the capture into Chrome's child process. In disposable sandbox `9f9154951ed343389a01c909e7eac430`, Chrome PID 786 inherited FD 3 as `socket:[2898]` and FD 4 as `socket:[2900]`; the bridge's parent FDs 26 and 30 were their corresponding spawned stdio streams (`socket:[2897]` and `socket:[2899]`). The child was `R`, with one thread and 1.1→2.8 MiB RSS over the first 250 ms. The 39-byte command write callback completed in 798 ms, but the output stream had no data chunk before the 30-second timeout (43.7 seconds observed) and session cleanup. This rules out missing child FDs, but not a long Chrome initialization. An earlier minimal GUI probe on the same candidate eventually got `Browser.getVersion` at 201.5 seconds, so the next discriminator is a no-ptrace sample with a 390-second bridge command window; do not interpret short-timeout clones as proof that Chrome never responds. This clone was deleted successfully. Host trace: `~/.local/share/csa-cube-node/build-logs/template-chrome-threads-2026-11-26T06-40-41-500Z-0f7d9b2d-63df-452b-afdc-ac3f395776bc.jsonl`.

### 2026-11-26 Chrome timeout contract and 300-second pipe sample

- The first attempted 420-second sample (`f3fa664e-b6c4-4170-9d5c-f83e93aaa81e`, clone `ee9ab00d92bf414b80ec750c7245a947`) did not test Chrome. The desktop launcher translated 420 seconds to a 390000 ms pipe-command timeout, while the Node bridge rejected values above 360000 ms. That mismatch made the bridge exit before spawning Chrome. The desktop timeout allows up to 3600 seconds, so the bridge validator is now aligned to the resulting 3570000 ms maximum.
- A 60-second calibration (`6845672a-a568-485e-b333-5ef08dc19379`, clone `3db92c11f860482385a436324b4f6cbc`) did start Chrome. The bridge listened on 9222 and answered `/json/version` with HTTP 503 while waiting for Chrome. Its 39-byte `Browser.getVersion` write completed, but the 30-second command deadline expired without a response. The initial log collector then hung in the SDK file-read API, which has no per-call timeout; after 13 minutes the creator process was idle with an open data-plane socket. The clone was verified by its diagnostic run metadata, deleted through Cube control API, and the orphaned local collector was stopped. No tenant sandbox was touched.
- The collector now tails guest logs with `commands.run` and a 15-second inactivity timeout, aborts after three consecutive read failures, and proceeds to bounded cleanup. TypeScript typecheck, bridge Node syntax check, shell syntax checks and `git diff --check` passed.
- Corrected no-ptrace sample `57c61be2-b660-4d02-9387-5ce3f5d4050b` used a 330-second session timeout, giving the bridge 300000 ms for its first CDP command. In clone `a999b2d45417410e8bc594e38ad4ce37`, Chrome PID 996 inherited FD 3 and FD 4 as sockets; parent FD 26 wrote the 39-byte frame and its callback completed in 935 ms. The bridge served HTTP 503 during startup, then `Browser.getVersion` timed out at 301683 ms with no pipe response. Chrome remained runnable and its sampled CPU ticks rose during the wait. The HTTP probe later timed out or reset when the bridge shut down. Websockify and system D-Bus had started. The bridge shut down cleanly after the timeout, without the previous unhandled `ECONNRESET`; the product desktop session failed readiness and the runner deleted the clone successfully.
- Chrome stderr contained a normal channel warning, `fpsimd not found`, and Crashpad errors opening absent `scaling_cur_freq`/`scaling_max_freq` sysfs files. No Chrome `FATAL` was captured, and current evidence does not establish these Crashpad messages as the cause. The host trace is `~/.local/share/csa-cube-node/build-logs/template-chrome-threads-2026-11-26T07-18-50-315Z-57c61be2-b660-4d02-9387-5ce3f5d4050b.jsonl`.
- `Sandbox.list` after cleanup showed exactly two pre-existing running sandboxes and one pre-existing paused sandbox; the diagnostic clone was absent. The candidate template and application template setting were not changed. Chrome/CDP remains unaccepted, so the template must not be promoted. The failure is in Chrome's startup/CDP response path; the DSH text task is a separate previously passing path.

### 2026-11-26 diagnostic-only Chrome background-networking comparison

- The prior minimal GUI pipe probe used `--disable-background-networking`, while the product desktop launcher did not. To test that single difference, run `1e593118-2d10-4e3a-8224-f06766a981d0` created disposable clone `af16340ea17f4ddd8d4bdec5a0790a9f` from READY template `tpl-f8982970681d4131becaa111` (2 CPU, 2 GiB, 8 GiB writable layer). The diagnostic runner added the flag only to the staged copy of `csa-desktop-session.sh`; the product script, image, template and app setting were unchanged.
- The bridge spawned Chrome PID 1022 and wrote the 39-byte `Browser.getVersion` frame. The write was accepted and its callback completed in 1609 ms. Chrome inherited pipe FDs 3/4; the bridge's parent pipe endpoints were FDs 26/30. No response frame arrived. At 300000 ms the bridge logged `cdp.command.timeout` (duration 308182 ms), then `bridge.failed`; `/json/version` alternated between `503 Chrome is starting` and no response. Bootstrap reported `websockify_ready=1 cdp_ready=0` throughout. The diagnostic watched for 360 seconds and took 402986 ms total including the state snapshot and clone cleanup.
- Chrome stderr contained only the `CHROME_VERSION_EXTRA` channel warning; no Chrome `FATAL` was captured. The final session snapshot records the Crashpad profile directory as missing, but this does not establish why Chrome failed to answer. Chrome was no longer alive after the bridge timeout cleanup, so that snapshot does not show a spontaneous Chrome exit. The `--disable-background-networking` comparison produced no timely CDP response and is not supported as a fix.
- The runner logged `sandbox.cleanup.complete`. A fresh `Sandbox.list` showed exactly the same three pre-existing instances: two running and one paused; the diagnostic clone was absent. The candidate template and configured application template were unchanged. The APFS Cube volume remained 110 GiB with 15 GiB available. Host trace: `~/.local/share/csa-cube-node/build-logs/template-chrome-threads-2026-11-26T07-32-17-071Z-1e593118-2d10-4e3a-8224-f06766a981d0.jsonl`.
- This comparison rules out the tested flag as a demonstrated remedy; it does not identify the underlying Chrome/CDP cause. Do not spend another multi-minute run repeating this flag comparison. Browser/CDP and noVNC visual acceptance still block template promotion; the separately passing Pi/DSH text tasks do not exercise this path.

### 2026-11-26 layered readiness and input verification

- The application now treats the tenant microVM, desktop, and browser as separate readiness conditions. Text tasks and task-file downloads require a connected tenant VM. The desktop readiness check uses the session identity, X11, VNC, and WebSocket listener. The desktop session records Chrome's CDP status separately and remains alive when Chrome fails. The desktop toolbar displays the browser status and can queue a browser-only retry.
- The isolated application acceptance used a disposable candidate-template VM and a random schema/user in the dedicated test MySQL. With the computer row `running/failed`, a real Pi `deepseek-flash` task wrote `vm-proof.txt` using Bash. The application downloaded the expected contents and returned 404 to the other test tenant. The task completed through the normal application queue. This establishes the Pi task and file path for this scenario; DSH and graphical Agent tools were not called in this run.
- A separate disposable VM replaced the Chrome bridge with a fixture that immediately exits. The app still reported desktop ready and browser failed. An authenticated WebSocket client received a 1280×800 RFB desktop and sent a click plus keyboard input to a Tk window in the guest. The guest window recorded the click and exact string `rfb-input-ok`. This verifies pixel and input transport through the application in the injected-failure case. Computer Use returned `Local browser access is unavailable for this chat`, so visual interaction with the product web page remains unverified.
- The browser-only retry endpoint was exercised against a test HTTP bridge returning the CDP discovery endpoints; browser state moved from failed to ready while the desktop remained ready. This verifies the retry/status control path with a fixture, not real Chrome recovery. A second tenant without a running desktop received 409 when requesting retry. The temporary VMs and database users/schemas were deleted; a subsequent Cube list showed two pre-existing running VMs and one paused VM.
- A diagnostic fixture initially used a single 18-second guest command for X11 startup and timed out. Splitting launch from bounded status polling showed the Tk window taking about 15 seconds to appear. Logs are retained outside the repository in `~/.local/share/csa-cube-node/build-logs/readiness-separation-2026-11-26.jsonl` with sensitive fields and configured secrets redacted.
- At 20:11 CST, CubeMaster rejected a fresh diagnostic VM with `130597`; its scheduler logged `SysDiskUsagePer=80.17` against the 80% threshold. XFS `/data/cubelet` was 74% used. The oldest closed 100 MiB CubeOps rotated statistics log was copied, compressed, SHA-256 verified, and preserved under `/data/cubelet/.csa-node-log-archive/` before removing the original rotated copy. The next sandbox creation succeeded without restarting the node. The root partition remains close to the scheduler threshold and needs an operational log-retention solution.

### 2026-11-27 workspace safety, network, and memory diagnostics

- A second normal product-queue test used DSH `0.2.2-rc.2` and the real `deepseek-flash` API in disposable VM `4516bf325c6946e29970c39dbcc0d460`. With the computer record `running/failed`, the task used Bash to write and read a file, reached `done`, and the owner downloaded the expected 16 bytes; the other tenant received 404. The task took about 5 minutes 16 seconds, including transfer and installation of a 143.8 MB runtime bundle because the candidate image carried an older bundle key. The VM, random MySQL schema and temporary user were removed. The sanitized end-to-end JSONL is `~/.local/share/csa-cube-node/build-logs/dsh-readiness-e2e-2026-11-26.jsonl`. This does not exercise Chrome or graphical Agent tools.
- The former sandbox creation network policy contained `allowOut: ["0.0.0.0/0"]`. Cube evaluates explicit allow entries before its private-network deny policy, so this accidentally allowed access to the node's private address. A disposable VM comparison demonstrated that the wildcard reached both the node SSH port and `api.deepseek.com:443`, whereas `allowInternetAccess=true` with no wildcard blocked the node port and kept the public endpoint reachable. The two existing running test VMs were updated sequentially through the Cube SDK and independently reprobed with the same result; their control-plane records confirmed the policy was persisted. No other private target or full cross-tenant network suite was verified. New-VM code in this branch uses the corrected policy, but the running public application has not yet been restarted onto that code. Logs: `network-isolation-2026-11-26T12-24-08-568Z-a95be436-71df-4ff0-a9c4-3a94cba1acc5.jsonl` and `tenant-network-policy-2026-11-27.jsonl` under `~/.local/share/csa-cube-node/build-logs/`. [Cube v0.8.0 network policy](https://github.com/TencentCloud/CubeSandbox/blob/v0.8.0/docs/guide/network-policy.md)
- The node root partition later reached 83% while `/data/cubelet` was 74%; old CubeOps, CubeMaster and journal rotations accounted for a significant part of the removable root usage. Only closed rotated files older than six hours were compressed on XFS, compared against their original SHA-256, and unlinked after an inode/size/time recheck. A total of 557,843,150 original bytes was archived under `/data/cubelet/.csa-node-log-archive/`; the live journal and active logs were left in place. `df -P /` then reported 11,983,468 KiB used of 15,159,300 KiB (displayed as 80% after rounding). The archive manifest is `~/.local/share/csa-cube-node/build-logs/node-log-archive-2026-11-27.jsonl`. Root remains close to the scheduler watermark; this one-time preservation is not an automated retention policy.
- Two bounded runs compiled one static ARM64 C executable on the Lima node, verified identical SHA-256 after copying it into a disposable Cube microVM, and timed the same work. In the first run Lima versus guest timings were: integer loop 28.609/35.896 ms, 100,000 `getpid` syscalls 10.801/10.021 ms, 32 MiB first-touch 14.010/355.202 ms (8,192 minor faults on each), four protection-change/touch rounds 0.590/29.691 ms, and 10 fork/wait operations 1.032/44.225 ms. A second run reproduced the broad disparity: first-touch 11.998/431.139 ms, protection churn 0.589/34.980 ms and fork/wait 1.116/27.267 ms. Both ran with 4 KiB pages, but Lima used kernel `6.8.0-149-generic` with 4 online CPUs while the VM used `6.6.119-cube.bm.guest.001` with 2. Thus the observation includes kernel, VMM and nesting differences; it does not identify one of them as the Chrome root cause.
- In the second run, a process-local 32 MiB `MADV_HUGEPAGE` allocation showed `AnonHugePages=32768 kB` and 16 guest minor faults, but took 506.093 ms versus 431.139 ms for normal first-touch in the same VM. No global transparent-hugepage setting was changed. This is not evidence for a hugepage workaround. The disposable VMs were deleted. Logs: `vm-performance-2026-11-26T23-38-40-725Z-2c1b215e-ad17-40b7-928f-b095a2f6ce01.jsonl` and `vm-performance-2026-11-26T23-39-22-810Z-199734a7-9a0f-4ba5-b982-4662eb3fb7ba.jsonl`. The user confirmed no independent Linux KVM node is currently available.
- Cube's documented Host Mount was tested with separate temporary Alpha and Beta directories. The Alpha VM wrote as UID 1000, was killed, and a newly created Alpha VM remounted and recovered the file; Beta did not see Alpha's marker and Alpha did not see Beta's. All test VMs and exact synthetic host directories were removed. The initial immediate Beta create returned `130597`; CubeMaster logged `Filter/cpu ... quotaCpuFree:2000, cpuq:2000`. Retrying after eight seconds succeeded, so this sample was a transient CPU quota-release delay, not the earlier disk-watermark rejection. Logs: `host-mount-persistence-2026-11-26T23-40-45-111Z-ade04d58-8509-4b73-9cf5-b77d1198da8c.jsonl` and `host-mount-persistence-2026-11-26T23-41-49-237Z-ade04d58-8509-4b73-9cf5-b77d1198da8c.jsonl`. [Cube Host Mount semantics](https://github.com/TencentCloud/CubeSandbox/blob/v0.8.0/docs/guide/persistent-storage.md)
- The dedicated node now bind-mounts `/data/cubelet/csa-tenant-workspaces` at `/data/shared/csa-tenant-workspaces` and has an fstab entry ordered after the XFS mount. The previous fstab was preserved at `/etc/fstab.csa-tenant-workspaces.20261126T234341Z.bak`; `findmnt --verify` passed. A root-owned node helper creates only UUID-derived tenant directories. The new XFS-backed mount is verified across a real Lima node stop/start as described below; existing tenant root filesystems were not migrated. The XFS mount currently reports `noquota`, and the outer volume is not encrypted.
- The application branch now requires this workspace before creating a new VM, places only that tenant's path in the Cube `host-mount` metadata, and records a `create_pending` marker before dispatch. An uncertain create outcome retains the marker and stops a duplicate create; a missing saved VM retains its ID and token and returns a recovery error rather than silently creating an empty machine. Sixteen tests passed against an isolated random MySQL schema/user, including the two recovery scenarios. A real product create in temporary VM `4085ca1e58604f8996d6ec30338a0ec9` wrote a UID-1000 file through the XFS bind mount; after the VM was killed, the app retained its missing identity and returned a recovery error. A separately created VM `d9d4fd65e0c74a33809e40309c817e51` remounted and read the same file. Both VMs, the synthetic workspace, test schema and user were removed. Log: `~/.local/share/csa-cube-node/build-logs/product-workspace-2026-11-26T23-46-37-853Z-35ca5f65-6901-4198-9559-dc9f54357df3.jsonl`. This is a workspace persistence test, not package/rootfs restoration, application restart recovery, encrypted storage, or real browser acceptance.
- A separate restart check used synthetic files only. It confirmed there were no active Agent runs and that the expected two tenant VMs were `running` while a third remained `paused`; it paused the two running VMs sequentially, checked the 12 GiB lab-space threshold, stopped and restarted `cube-node`, and confirmed that the XFS bind mount returned with the same device/inode and its random marker intact. Cube API, proxy and registry tunnels recovered; the same two sandboxes returned to `running`, the third stayed `paused`, and guest probes after resume still denied the node SSH port while reaching `api.deepseek.com:443`. The marker was removed. This verifies node and mount recovery, not application task recovery, package installation persistence, Chrome profile/CDP, or browser login state. The bounded run took 60 seconds and passed: `~/.local/share/csa-cube-node/build-logs/node-restart-2026-11-26T23-52-45-907Z-9e6ab6c6-6532-4584-a32f-9e7340c763dd.jsonl`.

### 2026-11-27 first versioned application release and LaunchAgent recovery

- Release 0.1.0-dev.20261127.1 built the feature source and a main rollback snapshot outside the repository. The additive MySQL migration 4 applied successfully. The attempted LaunchAgent transition returned macOS Bootstrap failed: 5; candidate API and web health checks did not pass. No Agent task was active. The release script stopped at the first LaunchAgent failure before completing the web restart.
- The first rollback attempt returned the same bootstrap status. The API was restored from the retained main snapshot with the previous Pi and no-workspace-mode configuration; the existing web service was restored from its prior launch definition. Local API, local Vite proxy, public page and public health then returned 200. The migration remains applied and adds create_pending with a false default; existing rows and all tenant data remain intact.
- A separate candidate-process probe on 127.0.0.1:18080, with DSH and Lima workspace mode, completed Cube health, MySQL migration check and HTTP startup in 39 ms, then shut down cleanly. This proves the candidate code/configuration can start outside LaunchAgent; it does not identify why the first LaunchAgent registration failed. macOS unified log records the old services being removed but no explanatory error. Treat the LaunchAgent cause as unresolved.
- Release `0.1.0-dev.20261127.2` materializes each plist with the version's absolute source and web paths, records bootstrap status, checks that each job is registered, and waits for local and public health. This removes the candidate's symlink dependency and distinguishes the launchctl return code from actual service readiness. Its rollback uses the same absolute-path method. Failed release artifacts and state are preserved under `~/.local/share/coke-super-agent/`; repository `releases/<version>` directories contain only the deploy and rollback scripts.

### 2026-11-27 release 0.1.0-dev.20261127.2 public DSH acceptance

- The versioned release deployed successfully with `CSA_AGENT_RUNTIME=dsh` and Lima workspace mode. The deploy script reported migration 4, API 200, local web 200, public page 200, and anonymous API 401. Both LaunchAgents registered using version-specific absolute paths. Runtime build and rollback artifacts remain outside the repository; `releases/0.1.0-dev.20261127.2/` contains only deploy and rollback scripts.
- Through the public application API, Alpha submitted a real `deepseek-flash` task in existing microVM `running/failed`. DSH reached `done` and wrote `release-verification.txt` with a unique synthetic marker through Bash. Alpha downloaded the file and the exact contents matched. Beta received 404 for both the task and its artifact. Run `6eabfae8-c07f-4a47-947c-2ef09929750c`, thread `569df67c-7be4-4eeb-951e-f6e96e59b5b5`; the full ordered trace remains in MySQL `run_events`, and a sanitized acceptance record is at `~/.local/share/csa-cube-node/build-logs/dsh-public-e2e-2026-11-27T10-15-18.jsonl`.
- This task transferred a 143,810,051-byte runtime bundle in 43.5 seconds and installed it in 76.3 seconds because the existing guest did not have a matching runtime key. `session.run()` then took 651,625 ms to emit its first `step/start`; its first Bash call completed in 42.1 seconds. The full task took 17 minutes. DSH stderr recorded the model-catalog request timing out at 5 seconds and falling back to its bundled catalog. A no-credential host request to `https://api.deepseek.com/models` returned 401 in 313 ms; a guest egress probe through the Cube command stream timed out at its 15-second SDK deadline before returning command output. These observations do not establish that model API egress caused the long first-step delay. The slow DSH startup needs a separate reproducible timing diagnosis.
- With no queued, working, or waiting tasks, the API LaunchAgent was kickstarted. Local and public health returned 200. After restart Alpha still retrieved the completed task and exact file; Beta still received 404 for its artifact. This verifies application-process recovery for completed task data and a host-mounted file. It does not verify interruption and continuation of an active DSH run.
- Both demo tenants reported VM `running` and desktop `failed`. CUA browser-state enumeration timed out after 30 seconds and reset its tool kernel; no product-page visual interaction occurred. Real Chrome/CDP, Browser Use, Computer Use, and same-screen user takeover remain unverified. Do not treat the passing text task as desktop acceptance.

### 2026-11-27 read-only review of Alpha Chrome startup logs

- The Alpha guest's retained generation 21 desktop log shows APT installed Google Chrome `158.0.8127.88-1`; the bootstrap later exited from `desktop-readiness` after tailing the desktop-session log. Chrome's browser process stayed alive in runnable state (`R`, 23 threads, about 123 MiB RSS), but `curl` to its own `127.0.0.1:9222/json/version` got connection refused. The readiness loop timed out after 360 seconds (368 guest seconds). The profile directory was writable, Crashpad directory was reported missing, and `/tmp/csa-chromium.log` was empty. This places the failure before a local CDP listener becomes available; it does not identify why Chrome never binds the port. No Chrome retry or reinstall was started without a demonstrated fix. Sanitized snapshot: `~/.local/share/csa-cube-node/build-logs/chrome-cdp-live-2026-11-27T10-23-10.jsonl`.
